                                                                                                                                                           2

     Public Disclosure


1    I.     Introduction and Purpose
 2   Q.    Mr. Trokey, please state your name, title, and business address.

 3   A.    My name is Robert L. Trokey. I am the Director of the Electric Section at the Georgia Public

 4         Service Commission (“Commission” or “GPSC”). My business address is 244 Washington

 5         St. SW, Atlanta, GA 30334.

 6   Q.    Please state your educational background and work experience.

 7   A.    My background and experience are provided in Staff Exhibit RLT-1.

 8   Q.    Have you ever testified before this Commission?

 9   A.    Yes. I have testified in prior Commission proceedings.

10   Q.    On whose behalf are you testifying?

11   A.    I am testifying on behalf of the GPSC Public Interest Advocacy Staff (“Staff”).

12   Q.    Please summarize the purpose of your testimony.

13   A.    This testimony identifies, from the Agreement for Power Generation between SF ATL2,

14         LLC d/b/a SF ATL2 (DE), LLC (“Serverfarm” or “Customer”) and VoltaGrid, LLC

15         (“VoltaGrid”), the service VoltaGrid has agreed to provide to Serverfarm. It also identifies

16         descriptions on VoltaGrid’s public website concerning its energy services and ability to

17         provide power to customers. Briefly, the testimony also considers potential risks to other

18         Georgia Power customers.

19   Q.    What documents did you review?

20   A.    I reviewed the Agreement for Power Generation produced as Bates No. VG003729,

21         including the Contract Summary, Contract Terms and Conditions, Statement of Work

                                                                                                     3

     Public Disclosure


 1         template, Campus Standards and Procedures, and form Lease Agreement. I also reviewed

 2         public pages on VoltaGrid’s website concerning Energy as a Service, remote power, utility

 3         microgrids, and power generation.


 4   II.    Contractual Description of VoltaGrid’s Service
 5   Q.    What service is VoltaGrid providing to Serverfarm?

 6   A.    The agreement describes VoltaGrid’s service as the design, delivery, installation, operation,

 7         and maintenance of a power-generation microgrid and the supply of power to Serverfarm.

 8         The agreement assigns VoltaGrid Power Delivery obligations, requires monthly power

 9         availability xxxxxxxxxxxx subject to contractual exclusions, places responsibility for the

10         Site and VoltaGrid Equipment on VoltaGrid, and contemplates operation of a gas-

11         generation power plant on the leased premises. These provisions describe VoltaGrid as

12         operating generating equipment to generate and deliver electricity for Serverfarm’s data-

13         center load.

14   Q.    What is the clearest statement of the service?

15   A.    Schedule 1 defines the Services as: xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

16         xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

17         xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

18         xxxxxxxxxxxx Bates No. VG003729, PDF p. 4 (Schedule 1, p. 1).

19   Q.    Does the agreement characterize power generation as an obligation of VoltaGrid?

20   A.    Yes.     The   definitions   provide:   xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

21         xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx Bates No. VG003729, PDF p. 7 (Schedule 2,

22         p. 3).


                                                                                                      4

     Public Disclosure


 1   Q.     Does VoltaGrid undertake an availability obligation for that power?

 2   A.     Yes. VoltaGrid commits to xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

 3          xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx subject to the stated exclusions and the MW level described

 4          in the applicable SOW. Bates No. VG003729, PDF p. 9 (Schedule 3, p. 1, § 1.2(a)).

 5   Q.     Why do these provisions indicate operation of a power plant rather than only the sale

 6          or rental of equipment?

 7   A.     The agreement does not stop at equipment delivery. It expressly includes operation and

 8          maintenance of the microgrid, imposes an ongoing availability standard, requires

 9          compliance with operating and air-permitting parameters, requires fuel reporting, assigns

10          commissioning and testing responsibilities, and provides that the leased premises may be

11          used xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx These are continuing operational

12          obligations associated with generating and supplying electricity.

13   Q.     Is your conclusion limited to what the cited sources state?

14   A.     Yes. Providing electric service is a functional description of the arrangement reflected in

15          the cited language: VoltaGrid provides and operates generation resources, supplies power

16          to the customer, and publicly describes a model under which the customer pays for energy

17          services or pays a dollar-per-megawatt-hour rate. Whether a particular statutory definition

18          or regulatory classification applies is a legal issue for the Commission based on the

19          complete record.

4      IV.        Statement of Work No. 1
5      Q.     Did you review the executed Statement of Work No. 1 between Serverfarm and

6             VoltaGrid?

                                                                                                         7

     Public Disclosure


 1   A.    Yes. Statement of Work No. 1 identifies the specific services VoltaGrid agreed to provide

 2         at the Covington facility. It requires VoltaGrid to design the microgrid, furnish and install

 3         generating and electrical equipment, operate and maintain the equipment, staff the site

 4         around the clock, manage specified natural-gas nominations, and provide phased Power

 5         Delivery reaching 90 MW. See Bates No. VG003796, SOW pp. 1–10.

 6   Q.    Does SOW No. 1 require VoltaGrid to design the power-generation facility?

 7   A.    Yes. The SOW assigns VoltaGrid responsibility for the xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

 8         xxxxxxxxxxxxxx The listed design work includes single-line diagrams, equipment layouts,

 9         protection settings, grounding and bonding diagrams, monitoring plans, an arc-flash study,

10         gas distribution internal to the power-plant node, and emission controls. Bates No.

11         VG003796, SOW p. 1.

12   Q.    Does SOW No. 1 require VoltaGrid to furnish and install generating equipment?

13   A.    Yes. The SOW requires the xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

14         xxxxxxxxxx including generators, selective catalytic reduction systems, transformers,

15         conductors, xxxxx protection and distribution equipment, an operations command center,

16         gas-pressure-reduction    equipment,     gas-distribution   systems,    gas   and    electric

17         interconnections, and auxiliary power. It also assigns VoltaGrid commissioning and testing

18         responsibilities. Bates No. VG003796, SOW pp. 1–2.

19   Q.    Does SOW No. 1 require VoltaGrid to operate and maintain the facility?

20   A.    Yes. The SOW expressly requires operation and maintenance of VoltaGrid Equipment;

21         onsite operators and maintenance staff monitoring the Services 24 hours a day, seven days

22         a week; controls and monitoring of the equipment and Services; emissions-reporting


                                                                                                      8

     Public Disclosure


1          documentation; customer-portal reporting; and gas filtering, monitoring, and metering.

 2         Bates No. VG003796, SOW p. 2.

 3   Q.    Does SOW No. 1 assign VoltaGrid responsibilities related to natural-gas nominations?

 4   A.    Yes. VoltaGrid must provide monthly daily nomination plans and nominate with

 5         Transcontinental Gas Pipeline Company the natural-gas quantities that need to be

 6         transported to the facility in accordance with daily requirements to meet the Power Service

 7         Objective. VoltaGrid must also communicate specified nomination changes and use

 8         reasonable efforts to balance receipts and deliveries. Bates No. VG003796, SOW pp. 2–3.

 9   Q.    What reliability and operational-continuity obligations does the SOW impose?

10   A.    VoltaGrid must maintain critical spares onsite, maintain regular maintenance parts and

11         critical parts, maintain xxxxxxxxxxxxx reliability subject to the contract, perform all

12         operation and maintenance through VoltaGrid and its personnel, maintain appropriate

13         redundancy, and resolve service failures under specified notification and response

14         requirements. Bates No. VG003796, SOW pp. 5–7.

15   Q.    May VoltaGrid use mobile or stationary generation equipment?

16   A.    Yes. The SOW states that VoltaGrid may use stationary or mobile power-generation

17         equipment to meet Phase Start Date requirements. Mobile equipment may be used as bridge

18         power while stationary infrastructure is commissioned, and all equipment must be capable

19         of meeting the Power Service Objective. Bates No. VG003796, SOW p. 7.

20   Q.    What specific Power Delivery does SOW No. 1 require?

21   A.    The SOW establishes xxxxxxxxx phases for cumulative export power of xxxxxxxxxxxxxx

22         xxxxxxxxxxxxxx and 90 MW. The phase schedule requires power at xxxxx, tested and

                                                                                                    9

      Public Disclosure


1           ready for service, beginning with construction-phase power and progressing to 90 MW of

2           prime power with N+1 redundancy. Bates No. VG003796, SOW pp. 7 and 9.

3     Q.    How is VoltaGrid compensated under SOW No. 1?

 4    A.    The SOW states Monthly Fees per kWh of xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

 5          xxxxxxxxxxxx, with cumulative Monthly Fees increasing as the installation scales. Bates

 6          No. VG003796, SOW pp. 9–10. This pricing provision is consistent with payment for

 7          ongoing Power Delivery under the SOW.

 8    Q.    What conclusion do you draw from SOW No. 1?

 9    A.    SOW No. 1 confirms that VoltaGrid’s undertaking is not limited to furnishing equipment.

10          It requires VoltaGrid to design and install the microgrid, operate and maintain the

11          generating equipment, staff and monitor the facility continuously, perform fuel-related

12          coordination, satisfy reliability and redundancy requirements, and provide staged prime-

13          power delivery up to 90 MW at xxxxxx to Serverfarm.

 4    IV.     VoltaGrid Website Descriptions
 5    Q.     Did you review VoltaGrid’s public descriptions of its energy services?

 6    A.     Yes. VoltaGrid’s website describes Energy as a Service, turnkey power and fuel solutions,

 7           microgrid power plants, and a remote-power model under which a customer provides its

 8           load profile and pays a dollar-per-megawatt-hour rate.

 9    Q.     How does VoltaGrid describe Energy as a Service?

10    A.     VoltaGrid states that Energy as a Service “provides customers with firm, reliable microgrid

11           solutions tailored to their unique energy needs—without requiring upfront capital


                                                                                                     12

     Public Disclosure


 1         investments.” It also states: “VoltaGrid’s microgrid solutions handle every phase of the

 2         energy delivery process, ensuring firm, reliable power without the need for CapEx.”

 3   Source: VoltaGrid, “Energy as a Service (EaaS),” accessed November 5, 2026: VoltaGrid Energy

 4   as a Service

 5   Q.    How does VoltaGrid describe its remote-power commercial model?

 6   A.    VoltaGrid states: “Our turnkey power and fuel services mean you simply give us your load

 7         profile and pay an affordable $/MWh rate — and we’ll figure out the logistics.” It also states

 8         that its trailer-mounted generators, energy-storage system, and switchgear can operate in

 9         configurations “to produce up to 38 MWe per microgrid.”

10   Source: VoltaGrid, “Remote Power,” accessed November 5, 2026: VoltaGrid Remote Power

11   Q.    How does VoltaGrid describe its utility microgrids?

12   A.    VoltaGrid states that its “Microgrid power plants are designed to seamlessly integrate into

13         any existing power grid.” It also states that “VoltaGrid can seamlessly supply the necessary

14         power to keep your grid operational until power is fully restored.”

15   Source: VoltaGrid, “Utility,” accessed November 5, 2026: VoltaGrid Utility Microgrids

16   Q.    How does VoltaGrid describe its overall business offering?

17   A.    On its home page, VoltaGrid describes itself as “[s]upplying intelligent, affordable, clean,

18         and turnkey power and fuel solutions for remote and grid-parallel applications.” It identifies

19         “Power Generation” as a technology offering and states that customers can lower capital

20         and fuel-consumption costs “with a single Power Agreement with VoltaGrid.”

21   Source: VoltaGrid home page, accessed November 5, 2026: VoltaGrid Home

22   Q.    What do the website statements add to the analysis?

                                                                                                      13

     Public Disclosure


 1   A.    The website statements are consistent with the contract. Both describe VoltaGrid as

 2         supplying firm power through microgrid generating equipment, managing the energy-

 3         delivery process, and using a service-based or $/MWh commercial model. They support the

 4         factual characterization that VoltaGrid provides electricity-related service to customers

 5         through generating assets it deploys and operates. I am not a lawyer and do not have an

 6         opinion as to whether, standing alone, they resolve any legal classification under Georgia

 7         law.


 8   V.     Conclusion
 9   Q.    What is your conclusion regarding the service VoltaGrid is providing to Serverfarm?

10   A.    Based on the cited contract language, VoltaGrid is providing an ongoing power-generation

11         and power-delivery service to Serverfarm. VoltaGrid is required to design, furnish, install,

12         commission, operate, maintain, and demobilize a natural-gas-fueled microgrid; satisfy

13         power-availability and power-quality requirements; manage fuel-related reporting,

14         permitting, and emissions compliance; and supply the generated electricity to Serverfarm.

15         The agreement assigns VoltaGrid continuing operational responsibility for a gas-generation

16         power plant and the delivery of power to the customer.

17   Q.    Does this conclude Staff’s testimony?

18   A.    Yes.

                                         Exhibit RLT-1

                               Qualifications of Robert L. Trokey


Mr. Trokey received a Bachelor of Arts degree in Economics from the California State University,
Chico in May 1993. In July 1997, he received a Master of Arts degree in Economics from the
University of Colorado, Boulder.

From August 1995 to August 1998 Mr. Trokey worked as an Economist and Chief Operating
Officer for Systems and Education Development International, Corporation, a Denver-based
management consulting firm. His duties included managing operations, accounting, conducting
economic research and developing business plans for international clients.

Mr. Trokey worked as an Auditor for Cable Audit Associates in Greenwood Village, Colorado
from September 1998 to July 1999. Cable Audit Associates represents various cable programmers
by conducting subscriber audits, providing accounting services and contract compliance testing.

From August 1999 to December 2000 he was employed by KeyBank National Association as a
Portfolio Monitoring Analyst, becoming the lead analyst for the Colorado Upper Middle Market
portfolio. Responsibilities included monitoring performance of commercial loans exceeding $25
million, collecting and analyzing financial statements and SEC reports, projecting and forecasting
financial trends, establishing risk ratings, reviewing asset-based lending documents, calculating
financial ratios and testing loan covenants.

Mr. Trokey began working for the Colorado Office of Consumer Counsel in January 2001 as a
General Professional III, Office Manager. As of August 2004, his position was reclassified as a
Budget Analyst II. In March 2006 he was promoted to the Rate/Financial Analyst III position.

In September 2007, Mr. Trokey started as a Utilities Analyst for the Electric Unit at the Georgia
Public Service Commission. He was promoted to Director of the Electric Unit in May 2020.
